_source line 4445 · 2026-05-22T08:54:29.769Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have more clarity:

1. **Steering v3 (PID 468898, tee → steering_llama_v3.log)**: 219 "Setting" lines = 136 baseline + 83 in alpha=-20 → alpha=-20 at 83/136 = ~61% done. Progressing well.

2. **Duplicate steering (PID 470295, nohup → /tmp/steering_107.log)**: 28 "Setting" lines. Since baseline has 136 samples, it's at 28/136 = ~21% of baseline. 

Both processes loaded the model and started running, each consuming 16GB on GPU 0 for a total of ~32GB on the 98GB card. The real problem is they're writing to the same output directory, so when the second one finishes it'll overwrite the first's results—wasted compute and a data integrity issue. I need to check the project config to see how gpu_ids are supposed to be assigned.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a complete picture. Let me also read lines around where the output is saved in steering_experiment.py to understand the collision risk.

Key findings:

1. **Steering v3 output**: Both processes will write to `data/llama31/steering_results/steering_results.json`. The second process (PID 470295) will overwrite the first (PID 468898)'s results. This is a data integrity risk.

2. **COMET Llama restart**: 40 back-translated entries logged, last at 780/1096. The process is at 83.7% CPU (running), but hasn't logged past 780. 

The validation has been stuck at 780 for hours — it started at 07:15 and it's now around 17:00. The back-translation step completed 800 samples worth of progress, but the COMET scoring phase that follows appears to be either extremely slow or hung entirely. ~9 hours), the estimated completion time would be around 18:45, so the process might still be running even though the log hasn't updated recently due to buffering. Since it prints every 20 samples and should show 800, 820, etc., if it's truly stuck at 780 for hours that would be concerning, but I can't pinpoint the exact timing from the logs alone. Let me check the other translation processes and compile the full status report.

There's a critical issue: two steering processes are writing to the same output file, so the second one will overwrite the first's results — I need to kill one immediately. On the positive side, the steering v3 fix is working correctly with 136 baseline samples completed and alpha=-20 at 61% progress, plus the hook execution shows no crashes. The agent also addressed my earlier concern about L18 by running the bootstrap MLP delta fix, which is progressing at 100/1000 samples with a delta around 4.95 percentage points.

However, the COMET Llama restart appears stuck at 780/1096 entries despite the process still running at high CPU usage. Looking at the baseline distribution across the 136 samples, I see a slight contextual bias already present (30.1% contextual vs 23.5% parametric), which will be important for interpreting steering effects once the alpha runs complete.
```

    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

   - `fix_version: "v3 (2D KV cache + return output in-place)"` — Agent noted the fix

So the registry is actually tracking the second process (PID 470287), not the first one via tee. The first process (PID 468898) isn't in the registry at all.

2. **Second process (PID 470295/470287, /tmp/steering_107.log)**: 113 "Setting" lines. Since baseline is 136, it's at 83% through the baseline phase.

3. **First process (PID 468898, tee → steering_llama_v3.log)**: 278 "Setting" lines, meaning it's completed baseline (136) plus alpha=-20 (136) and started alpha=-10 (6 samples). This one is significantly ahead.

4. **Output directory still doesn't exist**: Neither process has written results yet.

5. **Worker notification mismatch**: The worker said "worker_analysis_steering_107_20260522T082944 已完成（status=done）" but the registry still shows ANALYZING. The `on_complete` hook triggered at "2026-05-22T08:28:44.903019+00:00" while reproduce.finished_at is "2026-05-22T08:28:42.915Z" — this was the v1/v2 crash time, so something's off with the timing.

Actually, I see it now. The crashed process was marked complete, which triggered the on_complete hook and spawned the analysis worker. That worker analyzed the v1/v2 crash results, determined a relaunch was needed, and started v3 (PID 470287). The worker notification is about analyzing the crash, not the v3 results — the system is waiting for v3 to finish. So the first tee process (PID 468898) was likely the manual v2/v3 relaunch, and the second nohup process (PID 470287→470295) is the Agent's automatic relaunch after the crash analysis.

But wait, both processes are still running. Looking at the timeline more carefully, the Agent tracked PID 470287 at `/tmp/steering_107.log` — that's the nohup process started at 16:52 with `--position last`. The first process via tee at 16:45 doesn't have that flag, so these are definitely two separate steering runs. The Agent launched the second one as the official relaunch, but the first one (7 minutes ahead, already at alpha=-10) is also running. Both are writing to the same output directory, which could cause conflicts when they complete.

The key question is whether `--position last` actually matters — in v3 it's the default anyway, so they should be functionally identical. They use the same seed and parameters, so the results would be the same, but running two identical Llama-3.1-8B models on GPU 0 is wasteful and will slow both down. I should check the GPU memory usage and whether the report.md has been updated with the Agent's analysis.

Very important finding!

**The /tmp/steering_107.log** (the registry-tracked PID 470287/470295 process) shows **"Terminated"** at the end! This means the second steering process was killed. The non-Setting lines show:
- Model loaded, hooked at layer 28
- Started baseline
- Then "Terminated"

So the second process (registry-tracked, PID 470295) was terminated. It had 113 Setting lines before being killed during baseline.

But the FIRST process (PID 468898, tee → steering_llama_v3.log) is still running with 278 Setting lines! It's already past baseline and into the alpha loop.
 
The agent's analysis worker must have detected the duplicate and terminated the second process while letting the first one continue as the legitimate run. The "status=done" notification likely means the worker finished its analysis task, not the entire experiment. I should check if PID 468898 is still active, and it's concerning that report.md hasn't been updated to reflect steering_107 running.

Important: Only one "alpha = -20.0" line appears, and no Fisher/flip/distribution lines after baseline. This means alpha=-20 hasn't COMPLETED yet. The 278 total Setting lines = 136 (baseline) + 142 (in alpha=-20 loop). But wait, there are 136 test samples, and 142 Setting lines after baseline start. Does this mean alpha=-20 completed its 136 samples but the distribution line hasn't been logged yet? Or are there 272 total samples (136 conflict + 136 non-conflict)?


The log shows 136 conflict samples out of 272 total, which means if the script processes all 272 samples per alpha value, then the 142 Setting lines represent about 52% completion of the alpha=-20 iteration. Looking at how the experiment runs—baseline without hooks, then for each alpha value it registers the hook, loops through samples with resets, and removes the hook—I need to figure out whether the baseline only processed the 136 conflict samples or if it went through all 272.

The baseline distribution adds up to exactly 136 samples, so the baseline clearly processed just the conflict subset. But if alpha=-20 is processing all 272 test samples while baseline only did 136, then 142 Setting lines would mean we're partway through alpha=-20. The fact that there's no "alpha = -10.0" marker in the log yet suggests either alpha=-20 is indeed running through all 272 samples, or the log output is buffered and the next alpha hasn't been printed yet.

Given the pattern, it looks like each alpha value tests both conflict and non-conflict samples to isolate whether steering actually affects conflict decisions. At the current pace of roughly 23 samples per minute, the full experiment with 10 alphas and 10 control alphas processing 272 samples each, plus the 136 baseline samples, would take around 4 hours total—so we'd be looking at completion around 20:45 if we started at 16:45.

But I'm second-guessing the sample count. The baseline shows 136, which could mean either the script processes 136 conflict samples per alpha or 272 total (conflict plus non-conflict). If it's 136 per alpha, the total would be around 2856 samples and we'd be at 9.7% progress; if it's 272 per alpha, we're at 5% progress with 5576 total samples needed.

Looking at the progress more carefully: 278 - 136 baseline = 142 samples processed. If alpha=-20 handles 136 samples and we're at 142, that means alpha=-20 is nearly done and alpha=-10 has just started with 6 samples processed. But alpha=-10 hasn't appeared in the logs yet, which is odd.

I think the script might be running both steering and control directions for each alpha—136 samples in the steering direction plus 136 in a control direction for comparison. That would make 272 per alpha, putting us at about 52% through alpha=-20's processing.

Actually, looking at the code flow again: it runs all alphas with steering direction first, then all alphas with control direction. So each alpha processes 136 samples per direction, not 272 total at once. The 142 samples suggests alpha=-20 is complete and alpha=-10 has started, but the log entry for alpha=-10 might just be buffered and not flushed to disk yet. I'll wait for more data to confirm the script is running smoothly.

Now I should check if the first process is still running and look at what the Agent recorded in decisions.yaml.

But I'm seeing conflicting field values — the spec has one PID and log path while the top level has different ones, which is concerning.

The spec.pid now correctly points to 468898, though the top-level pid still shows the older 466385. The log paths also diverge — spec points to /tmp/steering_107.log while the top level references a different location. Looking back at my initial registry read, I only captured summary fields, but the steering_107 `updated_at` timestamp shifted from 08:28:43 to 08:58:19 — about 30 minutes later — confirming the Agent recently updated the registry in response to my report. The C3 MLP bootstrap worker is still progressing (650/1000 complete), so I need to check what's happening there.

1. **C3 MLP bootstrap**: The worker notification says "c3-mlp-bootstrap worker done", but looking at the logs:

   - `c3_mlp_bootstrap_llama_fix.log`: llama31_conflict_en_zh_L18 at 150/1000, ETA ~7627s (~2.1h). Delta ~5.03pp.


2. **No dedicated registry entry for C3 MLP bootstrap**: The C3 results are embedded in xnli_baseline_110 metrics under `mlp_contrast` rather than having their own experiment entry.
3. **The worker notification**: The Agent worker that was launched to handle C3 bootstrap monitoring completed its task, but the actual bootstrap computations are still running in the background.
4. **No new metrics in registry**: The `mlp_contrast` section in xnli_baseline_110 still shows the same qwen_conflict metrics without updates.
5. **Registry steering_107 update**: The Agent did respond to my report and updated to the correct PID.

The bootstrap jobs are still in progress while the monitoring worker finished, so the registry hasn't been updated with bootstrap CI results yet—D009's conclusion is still based on single-seed MLP deltas.

For the audit, I need to flag that D009 is vulnerable: Qwen's delta looks like noise at 2.4pp single-seed, and while bootstrap shows ~3.9pp, we won't know if the CI includes zero until it completes. Llama's L18 fix shows a stronger 5.03pp delta at 150/1000 samples, but that's also not yet validated by the full bootstrap.

I'm also noticing the Agent only partially updated the registry entry for steering_107—the pid got updated to 468898 but the log_path is still pointing to the old terminated process location instead of the current logs/steering_llama_v3.log file. And there's no registry entry for the bootstrap experiments at all, which means they're not being tracked as proper experiments with spec fields.

Let me post a report on the C3 bootstrap status and where D009 stands on validation.

The report.md is generated at 2026-05-22T08:28:44Z (about 40 minutes ago) and doesn't reflect current state at all:
- Says cuda:0 and cuda:2 are "idle" but both are running (steering and COMET)
- Doesn't list steering_107 in RUNNING table
- Doesn't show the COMET Llama restart or Gemma translate progress
- Still lists stale COMET killed alerts

This is stale. More importantly, I need to focus on the C3 bootstrap worker notification.


The worker_done notification indicates the Agent's monitoring worker finished, but the actual bootstrap computations are still in progress—qwen3_conflict is about 65% done with roughly 24 minutes remaining, while llama31_conflict_en_zh_L18 is only 15% complete with over 2 hours to go. The D009 decision hinges on what evidence currently supports the "C3 confirmed" status.

Looking at the preliminary gains: Qwen shows a 2.4pp improvement from the single seed, while Llama delivers a more substantial 9.2pp boost. Once the bootstrap finishes, I expect Qwen's delta to settle around 3.9pp and Llama L18 around 5.0pp—both larger than their single-seed counterparts. The critical question for validating D009 is whether these confidence intervals actually exclude zero. Given that Qwen's means are quite stable across lr and mlp variants, the variance should be relatively low, which suggests a 95% CI around the 3.9pp delta would be reasonably tight even with just 1000 bootstrap samples.

For Llama L18, the larger sample size of 272 makes the estimate more robust, so that CI likely excludes zero as well. The real vulnerability is Qwen—if its CI ends up including zero like the original asymmetry result did, then C3 would only be supported by Llama, which significantly weakens the overall claim. I should post an update clarifying that the bootstrap is still running despite the worker notification.

```text
Let me review the ROADMAP for issues:

1. **GPU 状态部分有错误**:
   - "cuda:0 — Steering v2 Llama" → 应为 **v3**（v2 已 crash，v3 是当前运行版本）
   - "plan_102, test ~100/316" → 实际已到 150/316
   - "COMET Llama train (780/1096, ~71%)" → 进程仍在运行但日志卡在 780
   
2. **CPU 任务部分有错误**:
   - "Qwen conflict 500/1000" → 实际 650/1000
   - "Llama 待修正 peak_layer" → 已在运行 L18 fix，150/1000

3. **plan_107 描述不准确**:
   - "修复崩溃后重跑中，预计 ~2h" → 实际已运行 ~30min，进度 358/~5576 (~6%)，预计 ~6h 而非 2h

4. **关键遗漏**:
   - 没有提到 PID 470295 被 Terminated 的事实
   - Registry 追踪的 PID 与实际运行 PID 不一致的问题（虽然 Agent 已部分修正 spec.pid）
   - C3 MLP bootstrap Llama fix 已经在运行（150/1000），ROADMAP 说"待修正"

5. **方向性审查**:
   - 研究方向合理：Detection (C1) → Characterization (C2+C3+C4) → Causal Intervention (steering)
   - D010 把 steering 升为最高优先级是正确的——C1 扎实，steering 现在可以成为重点

，C2 是 null result 的重新包装，C3/C4 则是支撑性工作

6. **时间与资源**:
   - ICLR 2027 截稿约在 2026 年 9 月底，还有 4 个月的时间
   - 4 张 GPU 全部分配给这个项目，目前利用率不错
   - Gemma 三模型验证和 DE 第三语言补充都是基于审稿人反馈的合理决策

7. **核心风险**:
   - Steering 是论文的关键差异化实验，如果 steering v3 的翻转率低于 15% 或 Fisher 检验不显著，整个论文的叙事会受到严重影响
   - C3 bootstrap CI 的结果也很关键

，Qwen 包含零值导致 claim 需要降级，COMET Llama 可能出现停滞影响数据质量验证

8. **ROADMAP 的信息缺口**:
   - Steering v3 baseline 的分布结果已有数据但没有记录
   - C3 bootstrap 当前的增长趋势（Qwen 约 3.9pp，Llama L18 约 5.0pp）
   - Bootstrap n=2000 的最新进展都需要更新

总体来看方向是对的，但状态信息有多处不准确或过时，steering 标注为 v2 而非 v3、ETA 严重低估都是需要修正的地方。

这些主要是信息级别的问题，因为 ROADMAP 方向正确，但 steering v2→v3 的标签和 ETA 不匹配值得警告。

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Representation steering via linear probe weights (plan_107).\\n\\nUses the linear probe's weight vector as a steering direction to causally\\nintervene in the residual stream during knowledge-conflict processing.\\nMeasures whether the intervention flips model output between parametric\\nand contextual answers.\\n\\nInput:\\n  - Model (Qwen3-8B or Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct)\\n  - Activation files (en_activations.pt from collect_activations.py)\\n  - Test data (en_conflict_test_verified.jsonl)\\n\\nOutput:\\n  - steering_results.json: flip rates, Fisher exact test per alpha\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport re\\nimport string\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\nimport torch\\nfrom scipy import stats\\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Probe weight extraction\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\ndef load_steering_direction(activation_path: str, layer: int):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Train a probe on the given layer and extract the steering direction.\\n\\n    The probe is trained with StandardScaler (matching train_probe.py).\\n    The weight is then mapped back to original activation space:\\n        w_orig = w_scaled / scaler.scale_\\n    so steering in the residual stream is geometrically correct.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    data = torch.load(activation_path, map_location=\\\"cpu\\\", weights_only=True)\\n    n_train = data[\\\"split_sizes\\\"][\\\"train\\\"]\\n    acts = data[\\\"activations\\\"]\\n    if acts.dtype == torch.bfloat16:\\n        acts = acts.float()\\n\\n    train_X = acts[:n_train, layer, :].numpy()\\n    train_y = data[\\\"labels\\\"][:n_train].numpy()\\n    test_X = acts[n_train:, layer, :].numpy()\\n    test_y = data[\\\"labels\\\"][n_train:].numpy()\\n\\n    scaler = StandardScaler()\\n    train_X_scaled = scaler.fit_transform(train_X)\\n    test_X_scaled = scaler.transform(test_X)\\n\\n    probe = LogisticRegression(\\n        class_weight=\\\"balanced\\\", max_iter=1000, solver=\\\"lbfgs\\\", random_state=42\\n    )\\n    probe.fit(train_X_scaled, train_y)\\n\\n    acc = float(np.mean(probe.predict(test_X_scaled) == test_y))\\n    print(f\\\"Probe accuracy at layer {layer}: {acc:.4f}\\\")\\n\\n    # Map weight back to original space\\n    w_scaled = probe.coef_[0]  # shape: [hidden_dim]\\n    w_orig = w_scaled / scaler.scale_\\n    d = w_orig / np.linalg.norm(w_orig)\\n    return d.astype(np.float32), acc\\n\\n\\ndef random_orthogonal_direction(d: np.ndarray, seed: int = 42) -> np.ndarray:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Generate a random unit vector orthogonal to d (control condition).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)\\n    v = rng.randn(len(d)).astype(np.float32)\\n    v -= np.dot(v, d) * d  # Gram-Schmidt\\n    v /= np.linalg.norm(v)\\n    return v\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-prod42/projects/clkc_mech_interp/ROADMAP.md\", \"content\": \"# clkc_mech_interp — Research Dashboard\\n\\n> 最后更新: 2026-05-22 09:05 UTC | 更新原因: Reviewer WARNING 修正：steering v2→v3, ETA 2h→6-7h, 进度数字同步\\n\\n## 核心方向\\n跨语言知识冲突信号的机制解释：残差流探针检测、层结构分析、因果干预。详见 paper_framing.md。\\n\\n## 当前阶段\\n**Research Phase**（MVP 2026-05-21 通过）。ICLR 2027 DDL ~2026-09-24。\\n\\n### 关键发现\\n- **C1 detectability** ✅ Llama 77.1%, Qwen 75.3%\\n- **C2 reframed (D008)**: 方向不对称性非冲突特有（XNLI ≥5pp），冲突信号编码在通用跨语言空间\\n- **C3 non-linearity (D009)**: MLP EN→ZH Δ 冲突有(+2.4~9.2pp) XNLI 无(-2.0~-0.5pp)。Bootstrap 进行中：Qwen ~3.9pp, Llama L18 ~5.0pp，待 CI 确认\\n- **C4 CKA (negative)**: 配对控制后差异消失，作为 appendix methodological lesson\\n- **Steering (D010)**: 最高优先级，probe-weight 因果干预\\n\\n### GPU 状态 (2026-05-22 ~18:00 UTC)\\n- cuda:0 — **Steering v3 Llama** (plan_107, PID 468898, ~7% done, ETA ~23:00 UTC)\\n  - Baseline: p=32, c=41, both=35, neither=28\\n  - alpha=-20 处理中，hook 稳定无 crash\\n- cuda:1 — Gemma ZH translate (plan_102, 150/316 test, ~47%)\\n- cuda:2 — COMET Llama train (780/1096, 进程活跃但日志无更新，可能 scoring 阶段)\\n- cuda:3 — DE translate Llama (plan_112, 610/1096, ~56%)\\n\\n### CPU 任务\\n- Bootstrap n=2000 (plan_111): loky 32 workers 运行中\\n- C3 MLP bootstrap: Qwen L23 650/1000 (delta ~3.9pp), Llama L18 150/1000 (delta ~5.0pp)\\n\\n## 近期任务（P0）\\n- [x] plan_110: XNLI 非冲突基线 — DONE, C2 reframed, C3 contrast confirmed\\n- [x] plan_104: CKA/SVCCA — DONE (negative, 配对混淆 M013/M014)\\n- [ ] plan_107: Steering v3 Llama → 运行中 ETA ~23:00 UTC (~6-7h total)\\n- [ ] plan_102: Gemma 全流程 → ZH translate 47%, 后续 probe + bootstrap\\n- [ ] plan_111: Bootstrap n≥2000 → 优化版 joblib 并行，运行中\\n- [ ] plan_112: EN↔DE → Llama DE translate 56%\\n- [ ] C3 MLP bootstrap CI → Qwen 65%, Llama L18 15%, ETA ~2h\\n\\n## GPU 预算\\n本项目分配: cuda:0,1,2,3 (westd-41302)\\nCOMET Qwen killed (RC1 resolved). COMET Llama 运行中但低优先级。\\n\\n## 关键决策日志\\n\\n| ID | 日期 | 摘要 | 关联实验 |\\n|----|------|------|----------|\\n| D010 | 2026-05-22 | Steering (plan_107) 升为最高优先级；C3 需统计加固但不作为唯一卖点 | xnli_baseline_110, mvp_002 |\\n| D009 | 2026-05-22 | C3 confirmed conflict-specific：XNLI MLP 对照证明非线性 en | xnli_baseline_110, mvp_002 |\\n| D008 | 2026-05-22 | C2 reframe：不对称性非冲突特异性，改为\\\"冲突信号继承模型一般跨语言迁移偏置\\\" | xnli_baseline_110 |\\n| D007 | 2026-05-22 | Dream 审稿结果处理：新增 3 个 P0 实验（非冲突基线、bootstrap 升级、EN↔DE | dream_20260522T023801 |\\n| D006 | 2026-05-22 | COMET 0.80 阈值不直接适用 back-translation：提升 plan_105 至  | comet_qwen_v5, mvp_probe_v1 |\\n| D005 | 2026-05-21 | 双模型 MVP CONDITIONAL PASS：Llama EN→EN 77.6% + Qwen  | mvp_probe_llama_v1, mvp_probe_v1 |\\n| D004 | 2026-05-21 | Llama MVP CONDITIONAL PASS → EXTEND：EN→EN 77.6%, 跨 | mvp_probe_llama_v1 |\\n| D003 | 2026-05-21 | 补采样至 2000 条 + 取消强制交集，每个模型用各自 verified 数据集 | mvp_probe_llama_v1 |\\n| D002 | 2026-05-21 | 采纳 Reviewer 5 条 warning，细化 MVP gate：增加 70-79% cond | — |\\n| D001 | 2026-05-21 | MVP 方案：EN+ZH 双语探针迁移实验验证跨语言冲突信号可检测性 | — |\\n\\n\\n\\n\", \"total_chars\": 2715, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Critical findings!

1. **Steering v3 progress is excellent**: 1652 Setting lines, 6 alpha values completed (alpha = -20, -10, -5, -2, -1, +1). That's 136 (baseline) + 6 × 272 = 1768 expected... but we have 1652. Let me recalculate: 1652 - 136 = 1516. If 272/alpha, that's 1516/272 = 5.57, so 5 alphas fully done (5×272=1360) + 156 into the 6th alpha. 

Looking at the log more carefully, I see alpha = +1.0 as the latest marker, which means alphas -20, -10, -5, -2, -1 are complete and +1.0 is currently running. So I have 5 finished alphas plus one in progress. With 1516 samples after the baseline, if 5 alphas consumed their full allocation, that leaves me with a partial count for the sixth alpha to figure out.

Testing the math: if each alpha takes 272 samples, then 5 alphas would use 1360 samples, leaving 156 for the current alpha—that's 57% through +1.0, which makes sense. The alternative of 136 samples per alpha doesn't fit the remaining count.

So the full experiment runs 10 steering alphas in each direction (the set [-20, -10, -5, -2, -1, 1, 2, 5, 10, 20]), meaning 136 baseline plus 20 alphas × 272 samples each equals about 5576 total samples. I'm currently at 1652 samples, which puts me roughly 30% through the run.

The steering loop is progressing smoothly without crashes, which is encouraging. The C3 MLP bootstrap for Qwen at layer 23 just finished—the MLP advantage over linear isn't statistically significant since the confidence interval includes zero, even though the point estimate is +3.84 percentage points. Now moving on to test Llama at layer 5, which was the problematic near-chance layer.

This is shaping up to be a critical finding for D009. The Qwen result showing no statistical significance, combined with the fact that Llama's layer 5 is operating near chance accuracy and likely noisy, suggests D009's claim about conflict-specific effects needs to be reconsidered. The layer 18 results for Llama are more promising and still running, but the evidence so far points toward downgrading D009's conclusion—what looked like a +2.4pp effect in the single-seed Qwen run was probably just noise, and the bootstrap is confirming that.
```
